### C4, roads: how they are built, and one deviation from the plan (2026-10-18)

Roads are the first real path network in the sim. What C4 settles:
- **You draw a road from point P to point Q.** It goes up the uplink with the usual lag as a `BuildRoad` order carrying both end points. The graph is integer all the way: nodes sit on the 2 m road grid, edge lengths are whole centimetres, and every tie falls to the lower Id.
- **The bill is paid up front, the labour is done by robots.** The order takes `RoadStructKgPerMeter` (2 kg/m) of Struct out of the stores in one go, or is refused and takes nothing. There is no haul leg. Each new edge is then a labour-only Build task for a Fabricator, at `RoadBuildMetersPerHour` (placeholder).
- **Roads and buildings keep clear of each other from both sides.** A footprint may not overlap a built road, a road being built, or the strip of a road order still in flight; a road may not run through a building, a site or a building order in flight.
- **Robots drive roads 1.6x faster.** A trip goes straight to an entry node, along built edges, then straight from an exit node to the target. The road is used only when it is strictly quicker than the straight line, by a speed-free integer test. A road demolished mid-trip costs the robot its speed bonus, not its way.
- **Both brains share one movement helper.** With no built road it is exactly the old `MoveToward` call, so a colony without roads is unchanged. There is no starter road in the default colony.
- **Not in C4 (follow-up):** demolishing and salvaging a road from the deck, edge marker posts, and a partial-fill look for a road under construction.

**Deviation from the plan, for the director.** The C4 row of the living-city plan says "era mode learns road distance and hauler throughput". It does not. Era logistics keeps its teleport model: material moves with no distance and no hauler capacity, so there is no road distance for it to learn. Era mode instead refuses to start while a road edge is under construction (the era band parks the fabricators, so it would never finish), and the agent/era ledger parity bar is checked with the roads finished first. Roads make the agent band quicker and so closer to era, never further. Teaching era mode distance and throughput would be a change to the whole logistics model, not to roads, and belongs with the cargo rovers (C6), when there is real traffic to be faithful to.

### C5a, districts and demand: what it settles, and where it chose (2026-10-19)

The design and its adversarial review are `docs/qa/2026-10-19/c5-districts-design.md` (the C5a section as amended by the review). C5a is data, painting, names, membership, a demand readout, the overlay and the City panel. It is inert until a district is painted: no existing suite paints one, so no existing output moves. Save version 37.

- **A district is a Kind painted on ground or on a floor.** Five kinds: Homes, Industry, Farming, Research, Civic. On the surface it is a set of 10 m lattice cells (the habitat bays' own lattice, a cell spanning [10k - 5, 10k + 5) m), painted as rectangles of at most 32 x 32 cells, every cell inside grid coverage. Underground it is a whole floor, which must be reached by the shaft. A cell, and a floor, belongs to at most one district.
- **The orders.** `PaintDistrict` (same Kind joins the one district it overlaps and keeps its Id; a rectangle over another Kind, or over two districts, is refused; ground already held is refused), `EraseDistrict` (the whole district; its Id is never reused), `RenameDistrict` (the text rides in a new `FString Text` on the command: trimmed, control characters dropped, capped at 32 characters, never empty, case-sensitive so "Gale" and "gale" are two names). Every verb rejects with a reason. An unknown verb is now refused too ("Unknown order '...'"): it used to fall through to the "executed" broadcast.
- **Names** are a hard-coded array of forty Martian place names picked by district Id, then `II`, `III`, ... after the forty. Not a data table.
- **One district per building.** A building belongs to the district holding its anchor lattice cell: bay 0 (the GridMin cell) of a habitat shell, the footprint centre of anything else; underground, its floor's district. A shell is never split: its bays and its residents belong to its building's district, whichever districts its bays reach into. A station module inside a bay is its own building and takes its own cell's district. A colonist belongs to their home's district. Erasing a district leaves no one homeless; membership is derived.
- **Membership is derived, lazily, and verified.** Two caches, rebuilt only when read: the ground (cell and floor to district, keyed on the districts' version) and the membership (building to district, per-district buildings, bays and residents, keyed on districts, zoning, colonists, furniture and the building set). `-RHVerifyCaches` re-derives both every time one is reused and logs an error on a difference. Beds and seats are not cached (rating and power move them every step): `GetDistrictCounts` reads them live. Beds are CERTIFIED beds, by the `GetHousingCapacity` rule (a rated floor's; a rated habitat's once the vault is certified); seats are the working stations' (rated homes only).
- **The demand readout is colony-wide**, a pure function of state, and nothing acts on it yet. Housing shares `GetImmigrationCount`'s terms (it is now `min(raw, room)` with the two halves public): settlers need `min(FreeBeds - ReserveBeds, ComfortCapacity - Population)`, the Big Arrival needs `FreeBeds >= BigArrivalFreeBeds`; the headline is the larger shortfall and names which term binds. Before the vault is certified surface beds lift the comfort term but not the free-bed one, so the free-bed term binds then. Jobs is the unemployed share and the seats short; Services is the coverage gap of every room function that earns Hope (the bar is the share of the Rooms bonus left on the table); Air is the share of residents in homes on short rations (any leg: the bar reads food or water shortages too, and the prose names the scarcest leg), with the oxygen margin in sols per home. Per-district rows show counts only.
- **A bug fixed on the way.** `CommandId` was never saved, so every loaded order was Id 0 and the deck's cancel removed the first queued order. It is now serialized, and a load raises the counter past the loaded Ids.
- **Deviations from the design text.** `EraseDistrict` takes an Id only (no erase-rectangle). The DISTRICT paint mode is a fourth tab beside ZONE / FURNISH / WALLS, and the City panel carries the same kind chips. The paint ghost and the district overlay are debug-draw and a procedural mesh like the roads: the wash is the glass material (`M_DomeGlass`) driven as a matte tint.


### C5b, growth: what it settles, and where it chose (2026-10-19)

The design is `docs/qa/2026-10-19/c5-districts-design.md`, section C5b-R (it replaces the original C5b, which the adversarial review blocked: growth issuing uplink orders built by robots plus an instant path in era would lock out 60x, break band parity, make the reserve uncheckable and leave colony orders unidentifiable after a load). Save v38. Inside HOME and CIVIC districts the colony builds for itself (the Q2a ruling); the player still places production, power, life support and anything special.

- **Growth is a project record, not an order.** `FRHGrowthProject`: the district, the kind (SurfaceModule | FloorCells | CivicRoom), the target (a lattice rectangle and the network it extends, or a floor and cells, or a cell or bay and a room row), the bill (resource to kg, the air for the new bays or cells included) and TotalSols / RemainingSols. No uplink order, no construction site, no robots: CanEnterEraMode is never affected and the agent and era bands run the same code (`StepCityGrowth`, after `StepBigArrival`, at the same place in both).
- **When.** At each sol boundary (the first step whose end falls in a new sol, a threshold on the monotone clock) at most ONE project starts colony-wide: districts round-robin by Id from a saved cursor; a district with an open project or its growth switch off is skipped. No start while any home is on short rations or smoothed Hope is Failing. Homes need the city's Housing shortfall (`HousingBedsShort`) above zero, net of the beds already under way; Civic needs a coverage gap on a CivicService function, counting rooms already under way (so two Civic districts do not both build the same room).
- **The bill is whole and atomic.** Per resource: free = uncommitted holdings - the open quota's bill - its reserve (GrowthReserveStructKg 1,000 of Struct; GrowthReserveO2Sols 10 sols of the whole colony's breathing for Oxygen; none for the rest). Uncommitted is C4's rule: completed buildings on linked floors plus the pool, never a construction site's hopper. The whole bill is taken at the start, or no project starts.
- **What it builds.** Surface Homes: the next Habitat Bay module of at most 2 x 2 bays on free lattice cells inside the district, edge-adjacent to a RATED, not door-sealed network with a bay in the district, touching no other shell, every bay centre covered, off roads and buildings; candidates by squared lattice distance to the network root, then (X, Y), the larger module first at one anchor. Underground Homes: GrowthCellsPerProject (4) cells on a rated floor, carved directly. Both are zoned Living and given GrowthBunksPerHome (2) bunk beds a bay or cell. Civic: the worst-covered CivicService function (ties by lexical name) in a free cell or bay of the district, furnished with its `CivicFurniture` recipe, after an adjacency check (nothing within two steps emits what the new room refuses or refuses what it emits); a cell is carved if a Civic floor has none free. A CivicService row must never emit Odor (checked at boot, refused when planning).
- **Done.** RemainingSols counts down linearly. In one step the site is re-checked (placement without the material check), then the building is added, the cells carved, the rooms zoned and furnished directly, and the new air filled from the bill so the home stays rated. A site that no longer fits refunds the whole bill; so does erasing the district.
- **Data.** New RH_Rooms columns `CivicService` (TRUE for Dining and Infirmary) and `CivicFurniture`; seven Growth rows in RH_Config (the six asked for plus GrowthBunksPerHome).
- **Deviations and open points, for the director.** (1) A surface network's air push is a flat `O2FillRateKgPerHour`, so past about 240 bays its leak (2 kg a bay a sol) outruns the push and it loses its rating on its own; growth keeps extending it (the rules say nothing else), so a big enough Homes district can grow a network out of its own air. A cap, or a push that scales with bays, is a follow-up. (2) Homes growth runs no adjacency check (the plan puts it on Civic only): on a floor the new cells land where the spiral says, and beside Gardens they cost Hope. (3) The growth switch is instant, like the machine switches, not an uplink order. (4) The surface adjacency term of the Hope index looks emitters up by position (twelve cells) instead of comparing every pair: the same pairs (the -scale sink digest is identical), and a Hope read with 500 grown bays and a hundred emitters drops from 0.42 ms to 0.13 ms.

### Growth versus Earth's quotas: the player's City / Earth share (2026-10-19)

C5b/C5c playtests showed growth and the quota ladder competing for the same Struct (bills rise x1.25 per round, about as fast as the Forge):
- sparing only the open quota gave a city of 117, with the third supply round at sol 104;
- also sparing the next round put the quotas back on time (sol 45) but stopped growth (pop 8).

The director chose **option a: the player sets a City / Earth share** in the City panel (default 50%), deciding how much of the colony's spare material grows the city versus the next quota. It is a deliberately political choice: build Mars, or pay Earth. Tuned by playtest so both the city and the supply line move at the default.

Also ruled: the City panel's "Air" bar is renamed **"Rations"**. It also fills on food and water shortages.

Queued with it:
- the Big Arrival's life-support gate becomes production-aware (net O2, food and water production must carry the new draw, not just stock on hand);
- settler ships get the same check.

### C5d, the City / Earth share and production-aware life support: what it settles, and where it chose (2026-10-19)

Save v39. It builds the director's ruling above.

- **The share.** `CityShare` in 0..1, a saved, colony-wide policy value, set at once (like the growth switch) from the City panel (five chips, 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 100% city, labelled "CITY <-> EARTH", placeholder D155) or `RH.Growth share <0..1>`. A new colony starts at the new row `GrowthCityShareDefault` (0.5).
- **The rule, as asked and unrefined.** Growth's free = held - reserve - the OPEN quota's whole bill - (1 - share) x the NEXT round's bill; between rounds the open-bill term is empty, while a quota is Open the next-bill term is. So share 1 spends everything above the reserve (C5b) and share 0 holds the next round whole (C5c). It is a pure function of state: deterministic, linear in time, and the same in the agent and era bands. `GetGrowthAvailability` reports the held-back bill (`QuotaBill`) and the whole one (`FullBill`).
- **The dial, 110-sol districts playtests (full cargo; C5b / C5c / growth-off for reference).** Q3 rounds met on sols; growth projects started; beds built (the city's target population); population at 110.

| City share | Q3 rounds 1-3 met | projects | beds | pop at 110 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C5b (no protection) | 36 / 54 / 104 | 10 | - | 117 (wave at 106) |
| 0% (= C5c) | 36 / 40 / 45 | 1 | 13 | 8 |
| 25% | 36 / 40 / 45 | 3 | 29 | 14 |
| **50% (default)** | **36 / 46 / 60** | **7** | **93** | 8 (see below) |
| 75% | 36 / 54 / 83 | 9 | 125 | 13 |
| 100% | 36 / 54 / 103 | 9 | 125 | 12 |
| 50%, lean | 36 / 46 / 59 | 7 | 93 | 12 |

- **Why 50%.** It is the highest step that keeps the third Q3 round at about sol 60 (60.45 full, 59 lean) and it builds seven projects against one at 0%. The ladder is a step function, so 40% and 50% give the same table and 60% already slips to 70. The default is also one of the panel's chips.
- **The unrefined rule is kept** because the playtests do not demand a refinement: both the supply line and the city move across the dial. Growth can only start between rounds while Struct is the binding resource (an open round holds its whole bill), so the cadence is a few projects per round, not one every few sols.
- **Production is measured.** The sim has no production estimator (the deck's and Hope's runway are stock over draw), and the machines' nameplate overstates what they make (an Electrolyzer on the day shift makes half its 213.6 kg a sol; the old lean colony made about 30-100 against a nameplate of 213). So the sim now credits production where it grants it (a finished Electrolysis or MeltIce batch, the garden's yield and oxygen) into a ledger, and reads the mean of the last `ProductionWindowSols` (3) COMPLETED sols (`GetProductionPerSol`). It rolls at the first step of a new sol, a threshold on the monotone clock, in both bands; a long jump records empty sols. Saved (the open tally, the ring, the sol it rolled at).
- **The life-support rule.** A leg (Oxygen, Water, Food) carries a head count when its production covers its draw (net >= 0) or its stock covers the deficit for `LifeSupportDeficitCoverSols` sols. Draw: breathing (0.75 kg a head) plus the standing leak of every rated floor and network, the water the recycling does not return (15% of 2 kg a head), food (0.62 kg a head). The Big Arrival needs the new check AND C5c's stock checks (kept as they were); a wave's own provisions count as food stock. Settler ships are capped to the most people every leg carries (closed form, `GetSettlerSupportRoom`), the log says when a ship is held, and the City panel's Rations line says so (D156). *(C5e then tightened the settlers' side: see below.)* The Rations prose covers the production case too (D153).
- **A deviation, for the director: the cover is 40 sols, in its own row.** As specified ("the stock must cover BigArrivalLifeSupportSols of the deficit", 10 sols) the check can never refuse anything C5c passes, because a deficit never exceeds the whole draw: stock >= 10 x draw already implies stock >= 10 x deficit. The old lean state (sol 85, 8 people, oxygen 30 a sol against 81, 1,510 kg in the pool) passes at 10 sols (1,510 >= 10 x 51) and is refused only above 29.6. So the cover has its own row, 40 (about one supply round, the soonest an Electrolyzer kit can come), and `BigArrivalLifeSupportSols` still sets C5c's separate stock check (its pin is unchanged). Make the cover 10 and production stops mattering.
- **What the playtests showed about the gate (not a tuning problem).** The scripted player farms two garden cells and runs one Electrolyzer, so the colony can feed about ten people. The wave is held on oxygen production at 75% and 100% (107 kg a sol made against 166 drawn at 113 people, 1,080 kg in the pool, the growth projects having spent the rest on new cells' air); at 25% it has no beds. With one more Electrolyzer, ordered when the City panel says the settlers wait on oxygen production (`RH_PLAYTEST_LIFESUPPORT=1`, off by default), the 75% run lands the wave at sol 90 and reaches a population of 117. At 50% the settlers (which the stock cover admits) outgrow the larder, which the bot never expands: 14 people by sol 64, food gone at 105, 6 recalled. That is the rule working as ruled (the stock buys the player forty sols to build farms), not a bug, but the director may want settlers held to net production only.
- **Open points.** The several-dozen population at 110 is not reachable by the dial alone for this reason: it needs the player's farms and electrolyzers. Settlers' provisions (the wave brings 50 kg a head, a ship's settlers none) are unchanged.

### C5e, settlers come only on net production; the playtest bot grows its life support with the city (2026-10-19)

The director's "yes to both" to the two follow-ups C5d left open. No save change (nothing new is serialised), no new config rows.

- **Settlers: net production, and the stores behind it.** A settler ship is capped, per leg (oxygen, water, food), to the most people k for which the leg's measured production covers its draw at N + k (net >= 0 after they land; the 40-sol stock cover of C5d no longer admits them), AND the leg's stock passes C5c's stock check at N + k (the O2 pool >= heads x 0.75 x `BigArrivalLifeSupportSols`; the food and water runways at heads >= `FoodRunwayFloorSols`; settlers land with no provisions). Both must hold; the ship brings the least of the three legs. `GetSettlerLegRoom(Resource)` returns the two rooms, `GetSettlerSupportRoom()` the minimum; the closed form has a 1e-9-head guard so production equal to the draw does not lose a person to rounding. The Big Arrival is untouched (stock legs plus production with the 40-sol cover). **A reading, for the director:** "keep the stock legs too" was taken as C5c's Big Arrival stock checks applied to N + k; before C5e settlers had no stock leg of their own (only the C5d cover).
- **The City panel note (D156)** now says what holds the ship: `The next ship's settlers are held to %d: they come only on what the colony already makes, and its oxygen / water / food production would not carry them.` (`Its ... stores are also too thin.` when a store is thin too; `the colony's ... stores are too thin to back them` when only a store is.) `FRHCityDemand` gains `bSettlersShortAir/Food/Water` (production short) and `bSettlersShortStores`.
- **The bot's life support (default whenever districts are painted; `RH_PLAYTEST_LIFESUPPORT=0` / `lifesupport=0` switches it off; runs without districts are untouched).** Once a sol it plans for the people the next two settler ships would make (or N + the wave while the City panel says the Big Arrival waits on supply) and builds what the three legs lack, through player orders only:
  - Oxygen: an Electrolyzer at a time while the oxygen made is under what those people draw plus what the open Earth quota still wants of it by its deadline (capped at one Electrolyzer's worth). The quota term is the bot's own addition: the first bot-only runs (garden floor, no quota term) pushed the third Q3 round from sol 60 to 89, because the garden floor's leak and the bore's hydrogen come out of the same oxygen the quota counts, and one Electrolyzer was the bottleneck of the whole ladder.
  - Water: an Ice Drill and a Water Plant a pair at a time on the surveyed ice (Ice_A, or Ice_B once drilled).
  - Food: a garden floor (-3: bored, carved, filtered, zoned Gardening Station, planting itself from the soil and seeds the ships bring). The cargo asks for the soil and seed pairs the missing cells need (a pair funds four cells; at most six pairs a ship).
  - Power: the solar kits (about 3 arrays an Electrolyzer, 0.7 a garden cell) and batteries owed for what was ordered ride the next ship; kits and banks go down on a grid inside the first pylon's reach. Power permitting: nothing that draws more is ordered while more than six kits are owed.
  - It logs a `LIFESUPPORT` line a sol (the books) and an order line for each thing it builds, and the REPORT block lists what it added.
- **Playtests (110 sols, districts painted, guide order; share = `GrowthCityShareDefault`).** pop at 110 / settlers / Big Arrival sol / recalled / Q3 rounds met / projects / beds / Hope:

| run | pop | settlers | Big Arrival | recalled | Q3 rounds met | projects | beds | Hope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 50% full | 124 | 12 | 90 | 0 | 31 / 34 / 44 / 55 / 67 / 86 | 9 | 141 | 53-100 |
| 50% lean | 120 | 9 | 88 | 0 | 32 / 36 / 43 / 53 / 66 / 85 | 9 | 136 | 53-100 |
| 75% full | 120 | 4 | 64 | 0 | 31 / 46 / 61 / 76 | 10 | 140 | 53-100 |
| 75% lean | 121 | 5 | 59 | 0 | 32 / 42 / 55 | 9 | 129 | 53-97 |
| 50% full, bot off (the C5d bot, new rule) | 10 | 2 | - | 0 | 36 / 46 / 59 / 77 | 7 | 93 | 53-100 |
| 75% full, bot off | 9 | 1 | - | 0 | 36 / 54 / 81 | 8 | 109 | 53-100 |

  At the default 50% the target is met: 124 people by sol 110 with nobody recalled and the third Q3 round at sol 44. How it is met: a ship's settlers are three at this size (floor((Hope - 70) / 30 x max(4, 0.2 N))), so the settlers alone reach 19 people by sol 90 (the sol the wave lands); the several dozen is the Big Arrival. The old bot under the new rule reaches only 10 and recalls nobody (under C5d's rule it recalled 6): the rule alone keeps the colony fed, the bot is what lets it grow. The quota term is what moves the supply line (rounds at 31 / 34 / 44 against 36 / 46 / 59).
- **Limits the playtests show (open points).** (1) After the wave the colony is under-fed: at sol 110 the 50% run makes 37 kg of food a sol against 77 drawn (the wave's 5,000 kg of provisions last about 60 sols), and 64 against 74 at 75%. Power is the brake: the bot throttles on owed kits (10 owed at the end), the ships bring at most two plus eight kits, and the power was short about half the run even before the wave; without the throttle the garden floor ran 70 cells and the lights went dark. (2) Water production is zero at sol 110: Ice_B is the last known ice and runs out (the alert says so); the bot's extra drill and plant sit on it. A third survey is not in the bot. (3) The Electrolyzer count and the garden's size are rules of thumb of the bot (about 100 kg of oxygen per Electrolyzer, 0.8 of a cell's nameplate food), not the sim's.

### C6, traffic: the director's answers (2026-10-20)

Design, professor review (BLOCKED), revision and re-review (CLEARED WITH CONDITIONS): `docs/qa/2026-10-20/c6-traffic-design.md`. Answers: **Q1a Q2a Q3a** (all three recommendations).

- **Q1a. Cargo rovers come from Earth** as a manifest item (`CargoRover`), like extra haulers. They are real sim vehicles that need roads.
- **Q2a. A commute is each colonist's own trip,** from their actual home to their actual workplace, by roads, open ground and lifts.
  - Districts group the trips for the flow picture and the traffic overlay. They are never an exemption: unzoned ends and trips inside one big district count.
  - The cost (Hope by the share of long commuters, and work time) phases in with population, from 60 to 160 people. So a crew-sized colony never feels it, and building a road can only shorten trips; it never switches a penalty on.
- **Q3a. Roads can be torn up,** with 50% of their Struct back, like buildings. A rover already on a removed road slows to a crawl and finishes its trip.

### C6a, traffic, the simulation half: what it settles, and where it chose (2026-10-20)

The design, its professor review and re-review, and the director's answers Q1a Q2a Q3a are in `docs/qa/2026-10-20/c6-traffic-design.md` (its "C6a, AS BUILT" section carries the build notes). Save v40, strict, **no new saved field** (a rover saves as any robot, an undeployed one in `ImportStock["RC_R"]`, a freight task as any haul; every commute, flow, freight and path cache is derived). C6b (shuttles, the TRAFFIC overlay, the demolish UI) is not built; its const accessors are (`GetCommuteFlowMatrix`, `GetCommuteFlows`, `GetCommutePairs`, `GetCommuteEdgeLoads`, `GetRoverStates`, `GetRoverRoadPath`).

- **Rovers.** `CargoRover` manifest item (3,500 kg) and the `RC_R` robot row on the one archetype. It deploys at the built node nearest the Lander (ties by node Id), or waits aboard under `ImportStock["RC_R"]` in both bands. Movement takes a profile (`FRHMoveProfile`): a rover plans with `SpeedMulPermille = round(1000 x 1.6 / 0.3) = 5333` and crawls at 0.3x on every non-road branch (no road, first and last leg, a missing edge). Freight = both ends road-served (a built node within 30 m), on the surface, 80 m or more apart, and a load over a hauler's 200 kg (the rover's 1,000 kg, or a consumer's 2-batch room). A rover claims the straight-nearest freight task it can reach by road (at most 4 plans); haulers take freight only after 300 agent-seconds unclaimed. Rovers dock only at road-served pads; the charge reserve is `DriveWh(3.0 x straight)`, checked at config load against the crawl (3.75 >= 3.33). A rover that breaks off a delivery puts its load back (`ReturnHaulCargo`).
- **Commuting (Q2a, variant a).** Every employed colonist is measured from their actual home to their actual workplace (roads at 4x, lifts at 60 m a floor); districts only group the trips into the flow matrix (an Unzoned row and column, nobody exempt). Hope loses `Ramp x 4 x min(1, 2 x long / pop)`; a long commuter's work counts `1 - Ramp x 0.15`, through one hook per job function (Lab and Workstation in the seat sums, Garden in the skill average). The population ramp is 60 to 160.
- **Road demolish (Q3a).** The uplink order `DemolishRoad` (spec in the design doc, C5): 50% Struct back for a standing edge, the whole bill for an unbuilt one.

**Deviations from the design text, for the director.**
1. `CommuteShaftCmPerFloor` 6000 and `CommuteLongCm` 30000 (the re-review's optional revert), not the REVISION's 2000 / 40000: the battery does not need the change.
2. The re-review asked for the demolish cases in `-roads` and `-traffic`. `-roads` is unchanged, because a new case there is a moved line under the commit gate; the order's cases are `-traffic` X10 and X8.
3. `TryClaimFreight` accepts a pickup within `RoverLastLegCm` of the rover without a plan (a rover standing at a site: a plan would say "no road beats the straight line" and refuse it). Anything further needs a plan that rides a road with both end legs within 30 m (R6 as written).
4. **R3 leaves an asymmetry for the director:** a rover that breaks off a delivery returns its load, a hauler still loses up to 200 kg on a forced charge (unifying them would move the battery).
5. `-RHCommuteAudit` measures the flows every step even where the ramp is closed (that is its job); it feeds nothing back and is off by default.
6. The headless agent band has no Mass ticking, so `-traffic` plays rovers through the brains' own sim calls; the brains themselves were proven live (windowless game: a rover claimed a freight task, loaded 1,000 kg of Struct, delivered it; forced to charge mid-delivery it returned the load to the Forge, reopened the task and drove to the road-served pad). The playtest bot does not buy rovers: it lays no road, so a rover would only wait aboard.
7. New dev cheats: `RH.Rover spawn|list|drain`, `RH.RoadDemolish`, `RH.RoadInstant`, `RH.Commute [flows|edges]`, and `RH.AddSolid ... out`.
8. The `-scale` flows pass was first measured at 1.5 ms (budget 1 ms) and 11 ms cold over 500 anchors (budget 10); it now looks each trip up once per distinct (home, work) pair instead of per colonist, and runs at 0.14 ms and 4.2 ms.
9. Gate-D rows D158 to D160 hold the new alerts and data copy; the Hope line and City panel line are C6b's.

### Step 2, going deep: Q1a Q2a Q3a Q4a (2026-10-20)

The director answered `docs/qa/2026-10-20/going-deep-plan.md` (gates D1-D8) with all four recommendations:
- **Q1a:** robots ride the lifts and work on any floor; a lift has a cargo capacity.
- **Q2a:** you paint the shapes (cells, tunnel lines, cavern boxes) on a floor's lattice, and the Borer carves them in order.
- **Q3a:** deeper floors are slower to dig and warmer (they need cooling power), but better shielded. Floors -1 to -5 keep today's numbers.
- **Q4a:** lifts and trams have capacity. A crowded lift adds a wait to every commute through it, counted from the flows; no car is simulated one by one.

### Step 3, economy: Q1a Q2a Q3a Q4a (2026-10-20)

The director answered `docs/qa/2026-10-20/economy-plan.md` (gates E1-E8) with all four recommendations:
- **Q1a:** Earth pays in credits. Unspent credits carry over, and each ship keeps a mass cap (ruling 7's packing feel stays).
- **Q2a:** Earth takes the quota. The cargo leaves on the supply ship and is paid for, so holding a stockpile no longer meets a round, and the City/Earth share becomes a real cost. This moves balance on purpose.
- **Q3a:** a Martian currency comes later, as a political act (the identity axis leaning Martian, or a Defy choice). Earth reads it as a step away; rivals may join it as a trade bloc.
- **Q4a:** no money inside the city. Money is the colony's books; wellbeing stays Hope, which is never spent (ruling 11), keeping poverty mechanics out of the mental-health framing.

### C6a sign-off: three deviations accepted (2026-10-20)

The director accepted all three points raised at C6a's report:
1. The road-demolish cases live in `-traffic` only, not in `-roads` too. Adding them to `-roads` would move that suite's log under the line-diff gate.
2. The R3 asymmetry stays: a rover returns its cargo on break-off, while a hauler still loses up to 200 kg as before. Changing haulers is a later balance move.
3. The playtest bot lays no roads and buys no rovers, so commuting is proven by `-traffic`, not by the 110-sol playtests (the longest trip was 120 m against the 300 m line).

### C6b, traffic, the presentation half (2026-10-20, not yet committed)

Presentation only (`Source/RedHope`); it reads the C6a const accessors and writes no sim state. Gate-D copy is rows D161 to D166.

- **Rovers** are a code-built kit model (`scripts/blender/rh_build_rover.py`, a low six-wheeled flatbed, 9.7k tris) imported with `rh_import_hs.py` under `Models2/Rover`, drawn by `URHAgentVisualizerSubsystem` with up to three crates on the bed while `CargoKg > 0`. The mesh has no separate wheels, so none spin.
- **Heavy-vehicle turning** (director feedback, live): a drawn road leg is the sim's waypoints with every corner filleted (`RHPathRounding.h`; 7 m radius for a rover, 1.8 m for a walker, smaller on short legs). The body heading is always the tangent of that path, drawn progress is arc length along it, speed eases to 0.45x into a 90 degree corner within a lag of about 3 m behind the sim, and a trip that starts facing the wrong way pivots on the spot (rover 110 deg/s, walker 260). Walkers drifted the same way on road legs (QA log: 93 samples, up to 169 degrees off their motion, against 2 samples and 32 degrees after the change) and now use the same drive. Shuttles ride filleted routes and linger through corners. Trade-off: a pivot at a trip start lets the drawn rover trail the sim by up to its pivot time (a rover's 180 degrees: about 1.6 s).
- **Shuttles and cars** (`URHTrafficVisualizerSubsystem`): instanced cubes, at most 400, seeded by (home district, work district, k). They are drawn only while the population ramp is open, because below it the sim measures no commute (there is nothing to draw and no cache is touched). The overlay does not force them on.
- **TRAFFIC overlay**: a button beside Map, a heat wash per built edge (separate procedural mesh, six material bins, since the road strip's material takes no vertex colour), a legend at the top left with the hover line. **Demolish Road** is a second card beside Road in BUILD > Logistics; one click transmits the order, the hint states the salvage first and the confirm line after.
- **C6a nits**: `LoadColony` resets `ColonistStations`; the demolish log no longer says "back at the Lander" without a completed Lander; the commute flows sort Ids and Dists together if the colonists are ever not Id-ascending. The 42-run battery diff against `battery_post_c6a` shows timing digits only.
- **QA hooks** (all off by default): `RH.Traffic overlay|status|noon|home|hover|demolish`, `RH.DeckScroll`, `rh.RoverFollowCam`, `rh.RoverTurnLog`, `rh.RoverYawOffsetDeg`.

### Step 4, territories: Q1a Q2a Q3a Q4a (2026-10-20)

The director answered `docs/qa/2026-10-20/territories-plan.md` (gates T1-T8) with all four recommendations:
- **Q1a:** regions off screen run in aggregate on the era integrator (the 5% parity bar); only the region on screen runs in full.
- **Q2a:** several settlements, each a real city with its own stores, power, people and Hope; one capital deals with Earth; they trade by convoy and are actors in inside politics.
- **Q3a:** territory is held by presence and treaty: settlement coverage claims ground, treaties draw borders, contested sites go to treaty or arbitration. Never by force.
- **Q4a:** convoys face weather, breakdowns and covert interference (delay or skim, costly if caught), protected by escorts, shielded cargo and waystation shelters. No raids, no attacks.

### Step 5, rising: Q1a Q2a Q3a Q4a (2026-10-20)

The director answered `docs/qa/2026-10-20/rising-plan.md` (gates R1-R8) with all four recommendations. With this, all five steps of the grand vision are planned and answered.
- **Q1a:** terraforming goes partway in one game: thicker air and less radiation, enough for towers and open-air greenhouses, never breathable. The underground city stays meaningful.
- **Q2a:** terraforming thresholds open each tower height class; heavy shielding can build one class early, at a price.
- **Q3a:** landmarks are a short authored list of unique civic projects (names placeholder), each counted once in Hope; some move the identity axis or rival relations; one per settlement or per planet, set per row.
- **Q4a:** the time-lapse can be watched any time from the City panel, and it also plays at the epilogue.

### Playtest bot plays the living city (2026-10-21, not yet committed)

Bot only (`Source/RedHope/Private/RHPlaytest.cpp`, `scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh`); no sim change, no data change, every action a player order or deck action. On by default whenever districts are painted; `RH_PLAYTEST_ROADS=0` (`roads=0`) returns the C5e/C6a bot **line for line** (proved: 110-sol districts run diffed against `c6a_full.txt`, zero differing lines; a 50-sol non-district run before and after, zero differing lines).

- **Roads** (once a sol, when the colony holds Struct beyond the open quota's bill plus the leg's own, plus 30 kg; each leg goes through `CanPlaceRoad` first). A footprint's half-extent is its FootprintX/Y in *metres*, and a road keeps its 6 m strip clear of it, so the base round the Lander leaves few corridors. Six legs, 223 m, 445 kg of Struct: a spine out of the Lander's east side along the Forge's flank (8,-2 to 22,-2), down to the industry end (22,-20), east to the solar fields (to 76,-2), north (76,40), to the second pylon's disc (78,78), and to the Ice Drill on Ice_A (126,108). A relay pylon at (44,10) is ordered with the east leg, because the Lander's 30 m ring and the first pylon's 40 m disc do not meet and every node must sit in coverage. Left unroaded, with reasons logged in code: the shaft head (the Borer is boxed in by the Lander, the Electrolyzers and the solar column; no corridor; every commute from it is a lift ride), Ice_B (700 m, 1.4 t, and its Water Plant stands beside its drill: nothing to haul), the dig site (beyond the ring). A leg refused for a reason other than Struct is dropped after eight sols. Solar and battery spots that a road now covers give way to the next free spot on three fields (`FindGridSpot`).
- **Rovers.** The sim sets a haul's freight flag only while a rover is deployed, so the bot watches the hauls themselves: a pickup and a drop 80 m or more apart, both within a rover's last leg of a built road. One rover once the mean of the last five sols' most-open candidates is at least 1, one more per 3, at most 3, at most one a ship; counted against rovers deployed, aboard and on the way. Logged as a `LIVING` line.
- **Water.** No third ice deposit exists: `RH_Deposits.csv` has Ice_A (30 t) and Ice_B (60 t) only, and the Ice_B alert itself says "The last known ice" (`HasUndiscoveredIce` is false). So there is nothing to survey, and extra surveys would only roll the rival-settlement discovery (50% a survey). The bot's change: no new Ice Drill and Water Plant pair on a deposit the alert calls low (it only burned what was left). The water at the end is production 0 against 35 kg/sol drawn with 40-45 t in store (see Open points).
- **Food.** Found by reading the books: (1) the garden's grow lights draw on the battery, all or nothing, and the bank was empty half the run (power short 41-56% of every run); (2) the plan's yield was the nameplate rule of thumb, 2.5x under the ledger (2.0 kg a cell planned, 2.05 made), so the plan asked for 93 cells where about 40 do; (3) the ships after the wave are too late for a wave that lands with 100 people. The bot now: plans on the best yield per producing cell the production ledger has shown (less a tenth, from six producing cells); counts the wave in the plan once 40% of its free beds stand and Hope's streak is half run (earlier was wrong: a 48-cell garden floor planted at sol 40 cost oxygen every sol and halved the city's growth); carves the garden ahead and loads the soil, seeds and banks on the ships in that window; loads Spare Parts first (six crates; a ship of pairs and kits alone left the stock at 0 by sol 50 and stalled the fleet), banks before extra kits, and fills with Solar Array kits (no Struct cost) only up to 64 arrays (the first uncapped version brought 119 and made ten times the load); and reads the colony's own mean generation against its load (1.25x) in place of the owed-kit count as the power brake.
- **Report.** The REPORT block gains `roads:`, `rovers:` and `at the end:` lines (roads ordered, edges built, metres, Struct; rovers ordered/deployed, freight loaded by rovers; water and food made against drawn, stocks, cells producing); the commute line already carried the long-commuter share and the Hope cost. `ROADS` lines log each leg and each refusal, a `LIVING` line a sol logs roads, rovers, open freight and the sol's mean power.
- **Playtests** (110 sols, districts, guide order; Q3 rounds met by sol):

| run | pop | recalled | Big Arrival | Q3 rounds | Hope | roads | rovers | freight by rovers | max long commuters | commute Hope cost | food made / drawn | water made / drawn |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 50% full | 117 | 0 | 75 | 31 / 43 / 48 / 59 / 72 / 92 | 53-100 | 10 edges, 223 m | 1 (sol 43) | 0 t | 0% | 0.000 | 100.0 / 72.5 | 0 / 35 (45 t stored) |
| 50% lean | 118 | 0 | 77 | 31 / 44 / 55 / 63 / 75 / 91 | 53-100 | 10 edges, 223 m | 0 | 0 t | 0% | 0.000 | 99.8 / 73.2 | 0 / 35 (40 t stored) |
| 75% full | 120 | 0 | 63 | 32 / 45 / 61 / 72 / 89 / 108 | 54-100 | 10 edges, 223 m | 0 | 0 t | 0% | 0.000 | 100.0 / 74.4 | 0 / 36 (41 t stored) |
| C6a, 50% full (no roads) | 124 | 0 | 90 | 31 / 34 / 44 / 55 / 67 / 86 | 53-100 | none | 0 | - | 0% | 0.000 | 37.0 / 76.9 | 0 / - |
| C5e, 75% full (no roads) | 120 | 0 | 64 | 31 / 46 / 61 / 76 | 53-100 | none | 0 | - | - | - | 64 / 74 | - |

  Pop is 117 / 118 at 50% against C6a's 124 / 120 and 120 at 75% against 120 (the 50% full run's settlers are three against twelve: the wave lands 15 sols earlier and the city's beds, not food, then bound; beds 132 against 141). Food made is above the draw in every run, which C5e/C6a's was not (37 / 77 and 64 / 74); every run meets six Q3 rounds (the 75% run's rounds are 32 / 45 / 61 / 72 / 89 / 108 against C5e's four).
- **Open points.** (1) **Water: no third ice exists**; 90 t of ice is all the data holds, the colony makes 700+ kg of water a sol while it lasts against 35 drawn plus the Electrolyzers' feed, and the stock (40-45 t) is what is left. Whether the economy wants a third deposit or a way to idle the drills is a design call (the breaker is a player action, but idling the drills sets production to 0 and holds the settlers' net-production rule). (2) **No freight ever moved.** The one long haul in this colony is Ice_A to its Water Plant (170 m); Ice_A runs dry about sol 40, which is also when a spare-Struct road reaches it (first spare window sol 29-33, the lane stood about sol 38), and the one ship in the window brings the rover at sol 46. The 75% run has Struct spare at sol 7 and still lays the lane at sol 33 (the relay and the east legs wait on the quota). Every other haul in the bot's colony is under 80 m or under a hauler's load. An earlier lane would need roads bought ahead of the first quotas. (3) **No commute ever gets long**: everybody lives and works at the shaft head, three floors at 60 m is 180 m at most (the run shows 120 m), against the 300 m line.

### D1, floors become places: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-21, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-21/d1-floors-design.md` (its REVISION overrides the original design and the professor review). Save v41, strict. Nothing visible changes: the battery is line-identical except the four named X15 lines in `traffic.log`, and the 110-sol playtest is byte-identical to the bot commit's.

**Plan deviation, told to the director: O1 is taken.** Furniture, seat, station and planted-cell keys stay `FIntVector` ordinals `(Level, ordinal, 0)`; there is no `FRHCellKey`. `going-deep-plan.md:35` said room, furniture and seat keys move to a lattice point. They do not in D1. An ordinal is a position in an append-only list, so it is permanent; re-keying waits for the first gate that can remove or reorder a cell. D2 must decide (review O9): refuse a shaft column on an already-carved cell, and no gate in step 2 removes a cell.

**Deviations from the REVISION (small):**
- `Debug_PaintCarve` refuses a floor that has no carved cell yet ("the first cell comes from the Borer or ExcavateFloor"). A floor in the table must hold at least one cell, because the save refuses an empty floor (the REVISION's `NCells < 1` rule).
- The `-deep` suite's `CellCentre` lambda in the -staff test (`RHSimCommandlet.cpp:1888`) swaps only `SpiralCell(i)` for `GetFloorCell(-1, i)` and keeps its Z of 0; `GetFloorCellCentreCm` carries the floor's Z and would have changed the surface refusal case's argument.
- The crew, district and colony visualizers' caches keep their count keys (floors are append-only, so a count is a complete key); `FloorCellsVersion` is exposed and bumped but nothing in D1 reads it, as the design said no sim cache keys on cells.
- `FRHUndergroundRegion` is not built: region 0's origin is `ShaftHeadCm` (review O3, adopted). The carve cursor is a per-floor `FRHSpiralWalk`, so the spiral rule costs O(1) a cell instead of an O(i) walk.
- `StepHabitability`, `PickFillHome` and the seat scan walk `FloorTable` (the floors that exist, shallowest first). The colony visualizer's per-level tile loop still runs -1..-MaxDepth (an empty floor costs one map lookup).
- Load: the floor block is read straight after the header and refused through `OutError` ("Save floor table invalid: ...", no Error line) before teardown. Room arrays longer than their floor, and rated floors with no cells, are repaired with a Warning after load.

**Tests.** New `-deep` suite (94 checks, in `rh_battery.sh`, 43 runs): K1-K10 and K6b of the REVISION, including the Borer carving a painted shape in the painted order, the adjacency grid against the hash fallback with cells carved past the last designation, and the growth peek equal to the cells carved.

### Gate-D framing review: final (2026-10-21, not yet committed)

The director approved ALL proposed wording of `docs/qa/2026-10-21/gate-d-proposed-wording.md` exactly as written, and ruled the nine mechanics of `docs/qa/2026-10-21/gate-d-mechanics-brief.md`: **F1b F2b F3a F4a F5b F6b F7b F8b F9a**. Every player-facing morale, sickness and recall string and icon is now reviewed and final; the standing "placeholder pending Gate-D" flag is lifted for the rows in `docs/qa/2026-10-15/gate-d-wording.md` (its Status column records each one). Four new strings from the mechanics below (the HOME AGAIN banner, the growth note at LOW, the quiet-colony line, the Lounge name and Notes) were written to the brief's examples and are NEW wording the director has not yet seen.

**Wording.** All 43 proposed rows applied verbatim with every format token kept: D4 D5 D8 D9 D13 D15 D20 D21 D23 D24 D34 D35 D36 D38 D44 D45 D47 D48 D49 D52 D54 D56 D57 D66 D67 D68 D72 D74 D80 D81 D82 D87 D99 D123 D125 D127 D130 D134 D136 D138 U3, plus D14 and D148 (below). D82 and D136 are CSV copy (`RH_ManifestItems.csv`), live-synced to the DataTable. D74 is a presentation-only mapping in a new header, `Source/RedHope/Public/RHOrderWords.h`, used by the confirm line, the uplink list and the refusal notice; the sim verbs are unchanged.

**The rulings.**
- **F1b.** The dispute pair is never someone born on Mars (`StepMoments`). With fewer than two Earth-born the beat passes quietly. Pastimes and join-ins still use everyone.
- **F2b.** A recalled Mars-born waits in orbit (a saved list of Id and name, oldest recall first). When a later supply ship lands with every home on a full ration and a bed free, they come home first, after the cargo and ahead of the settlers, with no Hope gate. Banner: `HOME AGAIN: %d born on Mars are back with their colony.` (one: `is`). **Save v42, strict.**
- **F3a.** Kept: the newest arrival is recalled first, the Mars-born last. The player sees only a count.
- **F4a.** Kept: births wait on THRIVING (smoothed Hope 75+), a bed and a larder. A carrot that never punishes.
- **F5b.** At LOW, growth keeps building Civic rooms and pauses only Homes. The short-rations pause is unchanged.
- **F6b.** The 0.60 work-pace floor holds after the Malfunction multiplier too (it was 0.42 at Hope 0).
- **F7b.** The quiet colony is a state, not an ending. The projected ending ignores it, the Earth and Crew panels show `The colony is quiet: everyone is safe in orbit`, the banner re-arms when people return, the epilogue clock pauses while it lasts, and no path is declared over an empty colony.
- **F8b.** The dormant Smoking Area is re-themed as a dormant `Lounge` (DisplayName and Notes only; the row key `Smoking` and its Function stay). The tobacco line is struck from `docs/habitat-vision.md`, dated.
- **F9a.** Kept: recycled water lowers Hope, soft and capped, with the fix named.

**Deviations.**
- **D14 and D148 are superseded, not applied.** The approved `The Quiet Colony` named an ending; F7b removes that ending (the `Collapse` enum value is gone), so there is no name to show. D148's `paused: the colony's Hope is low` named a pause of all growth; F5b ends that pause. At LOW the note reads `only builds services for now: new homes wait for Hope to recover` (the brief's phrase, with a lead-in so the City panel's `Growth %s.` frame reads true while Civic keeps building), and each Homes district says `new homes wait for Hope to recover`.
- F2b banner: the brief's example grammar is kept, with `is` for one child. A returning child keeps their own Id and name. A quiet colony takes them too (a ship over an empty colony with beds and full rations brings them home). The list has no cap (a cap would drop children, which is the loss F2b removes).
- F7b: the saved latch byte keeps its slot but its meaning is "the quiet banner has fired for this quiet spell"; it is renamed `bQuietAnnounced`. The extra guard that no path is declared over an empty colony is mine, to keep what the Collapse read did before.
- F5b: the Hope-band check moved into the Homes branch after the "housing is not short" check, so a Homes district that would not build anyway keeps saying so.
- The CREW tile is wider (92 to 124 px): `%d on short rations` (D4) did not fit.
- The two `-citygrowth` c5 checks are re-derived (five new checks, 193 to 198). All the F-checks live in a new `-framing` suite.

**Save v42, and W1.** F2b adds saved state: the recalled list sits straight after the colonists. v41 files are refused (X15 in `-traffic` and K6 in `-deep` re-pinned to 42 and 41). **W1, planned next, takes v43.** The midgame and late checkpoints are re-made at v42.

**Tests.** New fails-loudly `-framing` suite (44 checks, in `rh_battery.sh`, 44 runs): the Lounge and the two manifest copies in the live DTs; band and ending names; F1b (60 disputes with no Mars-born, a quiet beat with one Earth-born, pastimes unfiltered, a colony with nobody born here unchanged); F6b with the old floor shown and the multiplier kept above it; F2b and F7b end to end (recall order, the list, the quiet banner once and re-armed, the guards on short rations and on beds, a partial return, the save round trip at v42, a real supply ship at Hope 10, the epilogue clock held and resumed, no path over an empty colony). Mutation-checked: with the F1b filter, the F6b floor, the epilogue guard and the recall list all reverted at once, 13 of the 44 checks failed. Battery: 0 not green. Line diff against `battery_post_d1`, 89 lines, every one attributed: D5 2, D130 4, D87 2, D15 4, D34 2, F7b 10 (the banner and the old ending name, in crew, ending, percapita and water), F1b 18 (the dispute pairs, in arrivals and percapita), F5b 35 (`-citygrowth` c5), the version pins 10 (X15, K6), and one timing digit in arrivals. The pins `power: gen 50 W load 20 W` and `deposit Regolith_A: 40645 kg` did not move. 110-sol playtests with districts: full 117 people, lean 118, none recalled, the same as the bot commit; only the wording differs (the dry spell, WATER QUALITY LOW and the last-ice line).

### Era regolith parity (fix A) (2026-10-21, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-21/era-regolith-gap.md`, option A, as written. The era band now follows the agent band's routing rule for dug material, through one helper both ask. No save bump (the pile is already saved state), no data change, no balance change in the agent band.

**What changed.**
- **One helper, `URHSimWorldSubsystem::MayOverflowToStore(Resource, Level, SourceBuildingId)`.** Dug or produced material may go to a store (Stockpile or Lander) only when it is a construction material, or no completed machine on that level (other than the source) takes it as a recipe input, hopper full or not. This is the rule `8fa8299` put in the pile leg and D2a put in the output leg. Both legs of `StepTaskBoard` now call the helper instead of each keeping its own `bAnyConsumer` flag, so there is one copy of it. Behaviour of the agent band is unchanged (the playtest is byte-identical).
- **`EraLogistics`, the leftover branch.** After the hopper feed, the leftover dig budget goes to a store only when `MayOverflowToStore` says so. Otherwise it tops the deposit's pile up to `PilesCapacity_kg` (through `DigDeposit`, so it is clamped to the room, and the deposit falls by exactly what the pile gains) and the rest is dropped: it does not go to a store and it does not move on to the next designated deposit. That is the agent excavators standing by at a full pile. The ore not dug stays in the deposit.
- **W1's ice leg.** The helper is the hook: W1 calls it from the era output leg. This change does not touch the era output leg (option A2) and ice behaviour is unchanged. A home Ice Drill's ice still goes to the Lander in era until W1.

**Tests.** New fails-loudly `-eraparity` suite (19 checks, in `rh_battery.sh`, now 44 suites and 45 runs, so no existing log moves for it). EP0 the data. EP1 the helper (nothing consumes regolith or ice; a Water Plant takes ice but not regolith; another floor; the source machine itself; Struct always). EP2 an era step with no Forge sends the whole 96.77 kg to the Lander (the pin that already held). EP3 a Forge with a full hopper at night: the Lander and the hopper do not move, the pile fills to 500 kg in six steps and stops, the deposit falls by exactly the pile, a second designated deposit is untouched, and a pile 50 kg under its cap takes 50. EP4 the paired run: the agent leg's two excavators and two haulers are played by the harness through the same sim calls the brains make (the headless world has no Mass ticking), against an era leg of the same colony over 10 sols. Regolith dug: agent 6,300 kg, era 6,113 kg (-2.97%). Lander regolith: +0 and +0. Struct made: 1,500 and 1,475 kg (-1.67%). Pile at its cap in both. Mutation-checked: with the era branch put back to "always to a store", 7 of the 19 checks fail, and the era leg digs 19,355 kg (+207%) and puts 13.7 t in the Lander.

**Battery.** `bash scripts/sim/rh_battery.sh 10`: 0 not green. Line diff against `battery_post_gd`: 8 lines, no Struct line, both pins hold (`power: gen 50 W load 20 W`, `deposit Regolith_A: 40645 kg`). Each one explained: `-districts`, 4 lines (two pairs): the Dig designation line prints Regolith_A's remaining mass after an era stretch with a Forge on the level, 51 t before, 55 t now, because the surplus is no longer stockpiled. `-prefab` P2, 2 lines: 7 hauls became 8, still 26 steps. The harness plays the haulers, and the pile that era now tops up is one more thing to carry to the Forge. `-traffic` X16, 2 lines: the wall-clock digit in the message (2.1 s, 2.2 s). `-demolish`, `-icelow` and `-dayshift` did not move.

**Live paired run, Midgame re-cut (sol 28.35 to sol 39, agent at 16x, era at 60x, `RH.Ledger`).** Before (the C4 roads gate, 2026-10-19) and after:

| line | agent | era, before | era, after |
|---|---|---|---|
| solid Regolith, kg | 4,331 / 4,431 | 20,689 | 7,515 |
| deposit Regolith_A left, kg | 47,369 / 47,369 | 31,105 | 44,085 |
| Struct, kg | 3,948 / 4,350 | 3,973 | 4,400 |

(The agent column is the C4 run, then this run from the new Midgame.) The era run no longer over-digs: it digs 6.5 t in 10.65 sols (the agent 3.2 t; before the fix era dug 16.3 t more than the agent), and the Forge smelts the same 6.3 to 6.5 t in both bands (Struct +1,575 against +1,625, 3%; water, oxygen and hydrogen within 1.4%).

**What is left, and it is not the over-dig.** The 5% bar on solid Regolith and the deposit is still not met live: era holds 7,515 kg against 4,431, and its deposit is 3.3 t lower. The agent Forge draws 3.1 t of its 6.3 t from the Lander's stored regolith, and the era Forge draws none from it (era dug 6,484 kg and the stores moved by -16). That is the store-to-producer leg, which feeds a producer's input from a store when its output is short for construction, and the era band has no such leg (the gap note's "era never moves store regolith back into a hopper"). It is outside option A as written, so it is not built. It is the same class as the Struct overflow rule, and a candidate for the director: an era store-to-hopper feed for construction shortage (A3). The headless `-eraparity` suite has no construction shortage, so it holds the bar there.

**Playtests.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 rg_full guide full`: byte-identical to the Gate-D run (117 people); the lean run is also byte-identical.

### W1: distant ice (2026-10-21, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-21/w1-distant-ice-design.md` (the merged design and the revision after both professor reviews), with the director's corrections of 2026-10-21. Ruling "water a": the ice comes home RAW by cargo rover over built roads, from outposts beyond Ice_B. The first outpost is at **720 m, same bearing as the drafted 453 m site**; this is **distance option c, chosen 2026-10-21** (the bot's estimated Struct to open it is 1,749 kg, and the bot's own plan bill is exactly 1,749 kg). Save **v43**, strict (v42 is the Gate-D pass's).

**What changed.**
- **Data.** `RH_Deposits.csv` gains `Outpost` and `RegionId` (step-4 hook, 0 everywhere) and three hidden rows appended after Ice_B: Ice_C 80 t, Ice_D 120 t, Ice_E 200 t (Ids 7, 8, 9). `RH_Config.csv` gains `OutpostMeltMinCm` 30000, `OutpostHopperKg` 1200, `OutpostHintGridCm` 5000 and `OutpostDrillBufferKg` 1000 (each ending "Placeholder, director review"). `FRHDepositRow` and `FRHDepositState` carry the flags (not saved: re-derived by row name at load); `FRHTask::bOutpostSource` is runtime only.
- **Rules (sim, both bands).** A found outpost is drilled, never dug (the Dig verb refuses it; `TryClaimDig`, the pile leg and the era dig loop skip it). An ice-consuming building (the Water Plant) is refused within 300 m of a found outpost, and an outpost drill's ice is never delivered to one. `FindOutpostDestination` names the nearest plant (integer straight cm, ties to the lower Id) that is 300 m or more from the deposit, has room (Have + 200 under 1,200 kg) and passes the freight route test, with at most `RoverClaimPlans` plans a call. The output leg posts ONLY a rover freight task (an active rover, a live lane, a plant, more than 200 kg, or a final load of at least 25 kg once the deposit is empty), never a hauler task and never a store. Haulers never claim it (no 300 s fallback; re-derived at load). `RefreshFreightFlags` deletes an unclaimed outpost task that stops being freight; non-outpost tasks keep the C6 rule exactly. A halted or flat claimant does not hold the lane (`URHAgentSubsystem::IsRobotLive`). An outpost drill starts no batch while it holds 1,000 kg. Era moves a load only if there is an active rover, no fleet hold, a road-served pad and a plant that passes the same test, and never to a store.
- **Era A2 (the era regolith gap's open item).** The era output leg now reaches a store only through `MayOverflowToStore`, the helper from 6b2975a. A home Ice Drill whose Water Plant is full keeps its ice at the drill in era, as the agent band does. No battery line moved.
- **Finding and guide.** `GetOutpostHint` is the lowest-Id undiscovered outpost once Ice_A and Ice_B are found; its centre is the row rounded to a 50 m grid, so the scout's 60 m always covers it (worst offset 26 m). A once-per-row alert announces it (rebuilt at load, never re-fired). `GetIceExpansionStage` keeps every D2a value for the near deposits, then reads the found outposts: stage 1 carry the grid, 2 drill, 4 lay a road, 5 bring a cargo rover; stage 3 is never offered for an outpost.
- **Presentation.** The guide gains the two steps and the hint and outpost wording; the dig-site label reads "Outpost ice deposit"; the survey map draws the hint as a dashed 60 m ring. `RH.TerrainProbe <x> <y>` (read-only) reports the height field over the scout disc and the distance to the nearest peak or mesa base; `RHMarsTerrain::SceneryClearanceCm` records the outlines BuildScenery draws for it.
- **Bot.** `RHPlaytest.cpp`: `OutpostStep` surveys the hint once per row, plans one program for the first outpost found (pylons at s = 40, 110, ... m on a line 8 m beside the road line from (76, -2) m, one within 40 m of the drill, road nodes checked against every disc before any order), then orders one pylon at a time, the road in stretches of at most 120 m a sol over covered nodes, the drill once its pylon stands, and a Cargo Rover on the ship after the first pylon. Every order obeys the C6c Struct rule. `RH_PLAYTEST_OUTPOST=0` switches it off.

**Where the outposts are, and the scenery check (the spec's M9 risk 3).** Each site was checked in the editor before positions were pinned: `RH.TerrainProbe` over the 60 m disc and the nearest peak or mesa base, and a capture at `Saved/RHCapture/w1/`. A site is moved if a base outline is inside or within the 60 m disc, or the disc rises more than 3 m above the centre.
- **Ice_C (624, -360) m, 720 m ESE: kept.** Rise 1.31 m, nearest base 131 m away.
- **Ice_D: moved from (-20, -760) to (-100, -750) m (757 m).** At (-20, -760) the disc rose 6.73 m and a peak base was 43 m away. The new site rises 1.92 m, base 81 m away.
- **Ice_E: moved from (-700, 400) to (-400, 700) m (806 m, NNW instead of WNW).** (-700, 400) was inside a mesa base (-26 m), and so was every 50 m grid point near it. A sweep of the 780-840 m ring found (-400, 700): rise 2.62 m, base 108 m away.
- Captures: `w1_sites_00..03.png` (the drafted positions), `w1_probe2_00.png`, `w1_sweep_00.png`, `w1_sweepE_00.png`, `w1_final_sites_00..02.png` (the pinned sites). Every outpost lies beyond Ice_B (703 m).

**The change the -icelow log hides (shipped; Gate-D rows G4, D87).** The moment: Ice_A and Ice_B are both found and low, and the outposts are still hidden.
- Before (6b2975a): `ICE_B RUNNING LOW — about N t of ice left. The last known ice. Stretch the water: recycling makes it last, and the Water Plant restores it while ice remains.` and the guide showed no ice step (stage -1).
- After W1: `ICE_B RUNNING LOW — about N t of ice left. Survey for more ice farther out: outpost ice comes home by road and cargo rover.` and the guide shows "Find more ice" with the hint (stage 0) until all five deposits are found. D87 now appears only when every ice row is found and low or spent. `-icelow` keeps every line by marking the outposts found and spent right after I8 (`Debug_SpendOutpostIce`, which prints nothing); `-farice` F4 pins the new behaviour.

**Tests.** New fails-loudly `-farice` suite, 67 checks, in `rh_battery.sh` (now 45 suites and 46 runs): F1 the data (three rows, six old rows, Ids, config, the hint centre inside 60 m, every outpost beyond Ice_B); F2 the inert twin (20 sols with the outposts hidden against a twin without them: stocks, deposits 1-6, tasks, buildings and power bit-identical); F3 the hint, a real survey (61 m off finds nothing), the order, the rival roll (eight surveys, same rivals with or without the outpost finds), the alert once and not after a load; F4 the advice and the guide stages; F5 the refusals (Dig, a plant at 299 m and 301 m, no leak, forced designation); F6 coverage and a chain of ten pylons 70 m apart; F7 no store, ever (no rover, no plant, a plant still a site); F8 the destination (an unserved plant at 522 m skipped for the served one at 666 m, a plant inside 300 m, the hopper line, the plan cap of four); F9 the leg (240 kg first load, no hauler in 700 s, a load, a demolished edge, a Forge's freight task as in C6); F10 a halted claimant and a repair; F11 the buffer (30 sols, 1,000 kg, 15 W idle); F12 end to end in the agent band (2,205 kg of water in 3 sols, mass conserved to 1e-6 at every 100th step, the final 120 kg posts as one load); F13 the 1,200 kg hopper against 2 x Need; F14 the era gate (five blockers move nothing, a loaded case moves 1,000 kg) and the A2 rule; F15 the paired 10-sol ledger (the H2O ledger 8,559 kg in both bands, water melted 8,235 kg agent and 8,415 kg era, 2.2%); F16 v43 and v42 refused, and determinism. Mutation-checked: letting haulers claim outpost tasks fails 3 checks; dropping the outpost `continue` and the era store rule fails 13.

**Playtests** (`RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 <label> guide <full|lean>`, 0 sim errors). Before is the run at 6b2975a (`rg_full`, `rg_lean`).

- The bar passes in both: water made over 0 at sol 110, outpost freight over 0 t, and every ship after the first outpost load had Water production room for the settlers it housed (full: 2,891, 5,187, 4,736 against 3, 3, 0; lean: 5,442, 5,238, 4,687 against 3, 3, 0). No ship after the first load is capped by Water.
- Struct is what paces it: the program waited on 26 sols (full) and 15 (lean) against the C6c rule (the open quota's bill), so the road and drill stood at sol 64 and sol 54. The plan bill was 1,749 kg (10 pylons, 635 m of road, a drill).
