Against `battery_post_e2a`: exactly the design's move table - `-arrivals` 2 lines, `-citygrowth` 5, `-resupply` 5, `-ladder` 1, `-framing` 1 (the `Manifest: +` and `Ship launched` lines with credits) - plus the version lines (51 to 52), every save 72 bytes longer, E1's EC1 and EC6 texts, wall-clock digits, and the grown `credit.log` and `economy.log`. No CEO, cargo, resupply R-line or LADDER line moved.

**Playtest.** The default 110-sol districts run is byte-identical to `e2a_full` (66 `refused: Over budget` lines, as before). **The opt-in run** (`RH_PLAYTEST_CARRYOVER=1`, not a baseline): ship 3 arrives with 13,982 cr carried and loads 27,100 kg against a 13,207 kg award (the kits refused at sol 31.71 in the default run go aboard); after that each ship carries 89-383 cr over. Budget refusals fall from 66 to 3; no cap refusal; no rover deferral in either. Population at sol 110 is the same (115); food made is 70.1 against 74.3 kg a sol, because the quota sols move.

**Click test.** Three runs at 1440x860: 72 / 72 each (the picker's ADD now reads the sim's rule).

**-scale.** 0 errors after the fix's rebuild, every digest identical; the 80 Colonist Transports in 11.3 ms. (The run before the fix, at load 10-14, missed only the tram cold-commute budget, 10.05 ms against 10.)

**Checkpoints.** Re-made at v52: Midgame (sol 28.35) and Late (sol 62.35), 0 sim errors.

**Known gaps.** The deck has no "remove manifest line" (risk 6). The CEO line still says "kg manifest budget" (risk 3; E3). Two money scales stand unreconciled until E3 (risk 4: 1 credit per award-kg against E1's Earth values).

### E3 selling to Earth: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-23, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-23/e3-selling-to-earth-design.md`: the DIRECTOR ANSWER (**Q-A (a):** the quota shares the ship's 30,000 kg, loaded first; **Q-B (a):** a short launch 3 sols after a met quota, the shortfall added to the next round; **Q-C (a):** if the 110-sol colony stalls, the scripted player adapts first, then the repeat multiplier drops to 1.10, and both runs are reported), REVISION R1-R11, the PROFESSOR REVIEW, the design.

**What E3 builds.**
- **Met is HEAD's test; launch takes the bill.** `CanLaunchShip` (one rule for the sim, the deck and the bot): the phase, E2's credit re-check, the export cap (R6, only when sales are listed), then every line - quota + sale - against `Loadable` = uncommitted holdings (`GetGrowthHoldingsKg`: the pool and finished stores on linked floors) less the life-support floor (GrowthReserveO2Sols x people x each draw, for Oxygen, Water and Food; Earth does not honour the Struct float), with HEAD's trade tolerance. A refusal changes nothing and names the line, what can be loaded, the floor and any mass in sites or on unlinked floors. `LaunchShip` draws each line once through `TakeGrowthResource` (building-Id order, outputs before inputs, then the pool; sites and unlinked floors skipped), records E1's Out leg, debits E2's cost, then pays the sales through `CreditPay` (they fund the next ship) and clears the lines. The launch line gains `; to Earth %.0f kg (quota %.0f, sold %.0f) for %s cr`.
- **The short launch (R1).** `LaunchShipShort` from `QuotaMetSol + QuotaShortLaunchSols` (3): whole kg per line up to what can be loaded, export lines dropped unpaid, the shortfall saved as arrears that the next round's bill carries (a resource the next row does not bill gets its own line). Arrears belong to the next round while a short ship is inbound or the ladder rests, to the current round while it is Open or awaiting its manifest; a full launch loads and clears them.
- **The bills.** One rule, `GetRoundBills`, behind `GetQuotaProgress` and `GetNextQuotaBills` (C5c's identity holds): `ScaleQuotaBill`'s bills while the scaled total fits the configured cap, then the plateau `floor(raw line x cap / raw total)` (Q3: 18,987 / 9,113 / 1,898), then the arrears. `IsQuotaBillCapped`; the renewal line reads "bills capped at one ship".
- **Sales.** `AddExportLine` / `RemoveExportLine`: Mars-made rows only (`EarthBuys`, R9), at least 1 kg, a positive price, spare above the bill and the floor, within the cap beside the bill; the whole line re-quoted at today's price. Price = `V x llround(mult x 1000) / 1000 - L` in integer mcr, L = (50 g H2 + 300 g O2) a kg = 3 cr/kg; the multiplier is `GetRequisitionMultiplier`, so Earth's politics move what it pays for both the quota and the surplus. `SalesPaidMcr` is a part of the awarded total.
- **Owed.** While the manifest is open, growth's availability gains `Owed` (the round's bill line + the export line) beside the share's term: `Free = Held - QuotaBill - Owed - Reserve`; `QuotaBill`, `FullBill` and `bNextRound` keep HEAD's values; the growth refusal adds "owed to Earth at launch" only when something is owed.
- **Data.** `RH_Resources.EarthBuys` (TRUE for Regolith, Ore, Struct, Shielding, Ice, Water, Oxygen, Hydrogen, SpoilChemicals); `RH_Config` `LaunchH2GPerKg` 50, `LaunchO2GPerKg` 300, `QuotaShortLaunchSols` 3; and, by Q-C (a), `QuotaRepeatBillMul` 1.25 -> **1.10** (the C++ fallback too). CSV and DTs synced.
- **Save v53.** An export block after E2's credit block: the lifetime sales, then for every E1 slot its line's kg and locked price and its arrears (513 bytes today). It repairs with Warnings. **A second read-to-the-end fix:** E2's credit block now reads its rows to the end before judging them (an unsorted list no longer leaves the reader mid-block), and a block that cannot be measured stops the blocks after it from being read (`bCreditBlockLost`), as E2b did for the ledger.
- **The bot (5.2, R5, Q-C (a)).** A refused launch waits and is retried every tick; it goes short as soon as the window opens. While it waits, every order whose bill includes Struct is held (life support excepted) and placed when the ship has gone, and city growth is paused. The roads' and the outpost's Struct checks subtract `Owed`. A Parts Crate rides every ship. A second Forge, with a dig on Regolith_B, is ordered once Struct has held the program back for 3 sols (an outpost leg, a launch or growth waiting on it).
- **Presentation.** The picker's CARGO TO EARTH block (owed at launch, the outbound mass against the cap, a row per Mars-made resource with today's quote, its listed line, +100 and Remove); LAUNCH greyed by `CanLaunchShip` with the reason; LAUNCH SHORT once the window is open; "Earth collects the quota at launch." `RH.Export add|remove|list`, `RH.Launch short`, and `RH.Status`'s owed cargo, sales and arrears.

**Deviations.** XE1 pins the take-on run against its own take-off twin, not against figures from a pre-E3 binary (R7 asks for those; the met path is E2's code until launch). XE5's growth boundary (1 kg under and over with "owed to Earth") is pinned by `-citygrowth`'s Boundary lambda (R2), not again in XE5. The repeat's mechanics in `-resupply`, `-citygrowth` and XE8 are pinned at 1.25 by the harness (their ladder numbers), while `-resupply` R0 checks the shipped 1.10. `-scale`'s 1,000 launch checks ran on the scale city's 8 surface buildings, not the design's 200.

**Tests.** New `-export` suite, 25 checks (XE0-XE11, XE13). XE12's mutations, each built, run and restored by hash: drawing from a site fails 2 (XE2); no floor fails 2 (XE3); a float price fails 6 (XE6); the plateau from the scaled bills fails 2 (XE8); Owed inside QuotaBill fails 4 (first XE1). `-economy` 72, `-credit` 42 (their launching fixtures run with the take off).

**Battery.** 0 suites not green; both pins hold. Against `battery_post_e2b`: every `Ship launched` line gains its Earth clause (arrivals 1, citygrowth 5, credit, framing 1, ladder 1, resupply 5); `-resupply` R0 reads 1.10; `-citygrowth`'s R2 rewrites (b3, the b4 setup and its 10 boundaries naming the owed kg, the knob-0 line, the b5 rung, dial and instant dial; 21 lines each way, the rest re-positioned); the version lines (52 to 53); E1's offsets; saves +513 bytes. **R10:** city-growth lines after a met line appear only in `citygrowth.log` (intended) and, unchanged from the E2b baseline, in `deep.log` (K10) and `economy.log` (EC3), both fresh colonies with their quota still Open: no finding.

**-scale.** 0 errors, every digest identical; 1,000 `CanLaunchShip` calls in 0.93 ms (budget 5).

**Click test.** Three runs at 1440x860: 72 / 72 each. **Checkpoints** re-made at v53 (Midgame 28.35, Late 62.35), 0 sim errors.

**The playtests and the bar (Q-C).** The forecast's permanent stall did not happen: no ship was ever stuck, no launch went short, the longest wait was 0.2 sols. But the colony stops growing. Each launch empties the Struct yard, the next round's bill starts filling at once, and growth's Struct free was below zero on 98 of 110 sols, so the city builds almost nothing and the Big Arrival never lands.

| Run (districts, sol 110) | Ships | Q3 rounds met | Pop | Recalled | Big Arrival | Growth projects | Beds | Sent to Earth (Struct / O2 / H2 kg) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HEAD-equivalent (E2b) full | 8 | 6 | 115 | 0 | sol 98.01 | 7 | 115 | 0 (held, not taken) |
| E3, bot as it was, x1.25 | 5 | 3 | 8 | 0 | no | 0 | 17 | 10,431 / 4,775 / 994 |
| E3, bot adapted (Q-C (a)), x1.25 | 7 | 5 | 8 | 0 | no | 2 civic | 21 | 21,418 / 10,049 / 2,092 |
| E3, bot adapted, **x1.10** (shipped), full | 8 | 6 | 8 | 0 | no | 2 civic | 17 | 20,189 / 9,459 / 1,970 |
| same, lean | 8 | 6 | 7 | 0 | no | 2 civic | 21 | 20,189 / 9,459 / 1,970 |
| same, full, share 75% | 8 | 6 | 8 | 0 | no | 2 civic | 17 | 20,189 / 9,459 / 1,970 |

**The bar fails** at 1.25 and at 1.10: 0 recalled (pass), at least 3 Q3 rounds (pass), the Big Arrival by sol 110 (**fail**), the fleet (not instrumented in the bot's report; no halt alert, and every ship from Q2 brought 6-15 Parts Crates). Per Q-C (a) no further tuning is made here; both runs are reported to the director. The binding rule is growth's, not Earth's: while a round is Open its whole bill stands (the share applies only between rounds), plus `GrowthReserveStructKg` 1,000 kg, and under E3 the yard is emptied every ship. The levers the director might pull: the share applying to the open round, a smaller Struct reserve, a smaller Q3 (T3), or a growth budget of its own.

**Known gaps.** Fleet wear is not measured by the bot. `-scale`'s launch-check budget ran on 8 buildings, not 200. XE1 pins against its twin, not a pre-E3 binary.

### E3b: the share applies to the open round (director, 2026-10-24, not yet committed)

The director's ruling, option (a), on E3's failed bar: the City / Earth share applies to the OPEN round's bill too, not only the next one. Short launches stay covered by E3 Q-B (a), so the trade-off stays real.

**The rule.** `GetGrowthAvailability` (`RHSimGrowth.cpp`): `QuotaBill = (1 - CityShare) x FullBill` in every phase (it was `FullBill` whole while Open). `FullBill` is still the protected bill (the open round's while Open, else the next round's); E3's `Owed` (the manifest's bill lines and export lines while awaiting the manifest) is untouched and stands whole beside it; the reserves are untouched (Struct 1,000 kg, the oxygen breathing floor). `Free = Held - (1 - share) x FullBill - Owed - Reserve`. Same code in both bands; nothing is saved (no save change, v53 stays); no config rows. The `RH.Growth` cheat and the growth refusal text now say "(x% of its bill, the Earth share)" in both phases.

**Checks** (fail loudly). `-export` XE14 (4 new checks, 29 in all) and `-citygrowth` b5 (the two open-round checks rewritten to the new rule, one new boundary at 50%). XE5 now pins `QuotaBill = (1 - share) x FullBill` in every phase. Each XE14 check was proven by a mutation, applied by hand and reverted (export.log, 29 checks):
- Old rule (`bNextRound ? ... : FullBill`): XE5, XE14 "share 0.5 frees half" and "share 1 frees all" fail; "share 0 reproduces the old hold" still passes, as it must (at share 0 the old and new rules agree).
- Hold floored at 25% (`Max(share, 0.25)`): XE14 "share 0" and "share 0.5" fail.
- `Owed` dropped from `Free`: XE14 "share 1 ... owed cargo stands" and XE5 fail.
- `Reserve` dropped from `Free`: XE14 "share 0" and "share 1" fail (the oxygen floor and the Struct reserve).
- The short launch no longer records arrears (`Arrears.Add` commented out): XE14 "a short launch rolls the gap" and the three XE13 arrears checks fail.

**Battery** (`rh_battery.sh 10`): 0 suites not green; pins hold (power gen 50 W load 20 W, deposit Regolith_A 40,645 kg). Against `battery_post_e3` only `citygrowth` (24 lines), `export` (80) and `traffic` (2) move. Reasons: the growth refusal text gains "(100% of its bill, the Earth share)" (6 lines); the citygrowth b5 open-round checks are the new rule (6 lines) plus the new 50% boundary; export gains XE14 and its fixture log lines, XE5's wording, 25 -> 29 checks; traffic X16 moves only the wall-clock timing ("2.8 s" -> "2.1 s"). No sim number moves.

**Playtests** (`RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 ... 110 <label> guide full|lean`; x1.10 repeat):

| Run | Pop at 110 | Recalled | Big Arrival | Q3 rounds met | Short launches | Sols with spare Struct (of ~100 logged) | Hope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| pre-E3 HEAD (E2b) | 115 | 0 | sol 98.01 | 7 | - | - | - |
| E3 full | 8 | 0 | no | 6 | 0 | 5 | 55..100 |
| **E3b full, share 50%** | 8 | 0 | no | 3 | 0 | 8 | 55..100 |
| E3b lean, 50% | 6 | 0 | no | 3 | 0 | 9 | 55..100 |
| E3b full, 75% | 112 | 0 | sol 79.57 | 1 | 1 | 66 | 54..100 |
| E3b full, 25% | 8 | 0 | no | 6 | 0 | 8 | 55..100 |
| diagnostic: 50%, Struct reserve 0 | 113 | 0 | sol 77.01 | 1 | 1 | 79 | 55..100 |
| diagnostic: 50%, Struct reserve 500 | 113 | 0 | sol 80.01 | 1 | 1 | 64 | 55..100 |

"Credits earned" is not in the playtest report (the bot logs kg, not credits), so it is not tabulated. Sols with spare Struct count the bot's daily GROWTH lines with Struct free above zero.

**The bar fails at the default 50%.** Rounds (3), 0 recalled and the fleet running hold; the Big Arrival does not land and the population stays 8. Why: the open-round hold falls from 100% to 50% (Struct 1,250-1,664 kg held back in the middle rounds, the yard emptied by each launch), but the 1,000 kg `GrowthReserveStructKg` stands on top of it, so Struct held almost never reaches reserve + hold + a 640 kg project (8 sols of 99). The reserve is the remaining blocker: with it at 0 or 500 (diagnostic runs, config override only, nothing changed) the Big Arrival lands (sols 77-80), but Earth's rounds then slip to 1 Q3 round in 110 sols. At a 75% share the colony grows but meets 1 round; at 25% it meets 6 rounds and does not grow (E3's result). So the share and the reserve together trade rounds for people one for one, and no point tried passes both halves of the bar. Nothing else was tuned. The levers left to the director: the Struct reserve, a smaller Q3 (T3), or a growth budget of its own.

### Playtest bot builds industry by default (director, 2026-10-24)

The director's ruling on the Struct study: the playtest bot plays the industry, the study's `industry=2`, whenever districts are painted, the way it already plays the life support, the living city, the outpost and the night bank. The sim is untouched (no `RedHopeSim` change, no save change, no data change); only `RHPlaytest.cpp` and `rh_playtest.sh` move.

**What the study found** (commit 93646b0; 50% City / Earth share, 110 sols, the director's bar: 0 recalled, Big Arrival by sol 110, at least 3 Q3 rounds, no robot halted). The wall in the default run is one Forge: the bot's second Forge was refused, the single Forge worked the day shift at 88% of its 150 kg/sol ceiling, regolith was not the limit (the pile sat at its cap 83% of the time), Earth took 68% of the Struct made, and growth's free Struct was below zero on 91 of 99 sols.

| option | pop | Big Arrival | Q3 rounds | bar |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| default | 8 | none | 3 | FAIL |
| industry=2, full | 122 | sol 81 | 7 | PASS |
| industry=2, lean | 121 | sol 81 | 8 | PASS |
| Q3 Struct 1,500 | 108 | sol 101 | 3 | PASS, but a threshold; fails lean, under-fed |
| reserve 500 | 113 | sol 80 | 1 | FAIL |
| 75% share, any option | | | 1-2 | FAIL on rounds |

**50% is the balanced share.** At 75% the colony grows but meets only 1-2 Q3 rounds in every option tried (industry, the smaller Q3 bill, the lower reserve); at 25% it meets the rounds and does not grow (E3's result); 50% with industry is the one point that passes both halves of the bar, on full and lean. So the share stays at 50% and the bot's default moves to the industry instead.

**What changed.**
- `RHPlaytest.cpp`, the `RH.Playtest` argument parse: `industry` defaults to 2 whenever `districts=1` is given and no `industry=` is. Explicit `industry=0` (`RH_PLAYTEST_INDUSTRY=0`, which `rh_playtest.sh` already passes through) is the opt-out and gives the E3b bot back; `industry=1` and `indforges=N` are as the study left them. Playing the industry implies reading it (`indlog`), so the INDUSTRY line a sol and the REPORT lines (Forge time, the Struct ledger, the regolith chain, each robot class's wear, and `REPORT  fleet: peak wear seen on any robot N of 100 (halts at 100)`) are in every default run and the director's "fleet running" bar item is always measured. The start line says `the player builds industry (industry=2: ...)` when it is on. Without districts nothing changes: `industry` stays 0 and `indlog` stays off.
- `RHPlaytest.cpp`, `RH.Playtest ... roads=0` and the other opt-outs: unchanged, but a run that wants an older bot back line for line now adds `industry=0` too (said in the `rh_playtest.sh` header).

**The false report, fixed.** The bot's second Forge went to a fixed spot, (24, -14) m, that the spine road (laid at sol 6) had taken by sol 12; the sim refused it at sol 12.04 ("Footprint overlaps a road"), but the bot had already written the milestone `second Forge ordered` at sol 12.00 and the REPORT said `second Forge ordered at sol 12.00`. An order is only an order until the sim answers it (it answers on its next step, through `OnCommandRejected` or by placing the site). So:
- `RHPlaytest.cpp`, `FPlaytest::FForgeOrder`, `NoteForgeOrder`, `NoteForgeRefused`, `ForgeOrderStep`, `ForgeOrderText`: each second or third Forge order is followed from the order to the sim's answer to the finished building. The milestones are now `second Forge order accepted (...)` at the sol the site stands, `second Forge running` when it is built, or `second Forge order refused (<the sim's reason>)` with a `FORGE` line saying nothing was built and the bot does not order it again; the REPORT lines (`earth:` and `industry program:`) say `ordered at sol S, accepted at sol A, standing since sol B` or `ordered at sol S but REFUSED at sol R (<reason>): none was built`, and count placed / accepted / refused. The bot's behaviour is untouched: it still orders, and still does not retry, exactly as before (read-only bookkeeping; the sim lines of the industry runs are identical to the HEAD runs, see below).
- The other "ordered" report lines, checked: the industry program's "excavator(s) and hauler(s) ordered on ships" had the same mistake. The cargo list asks for them, the ship's budget refuses what does not fit (sol 29.46: `AdvExcavator refused: Over budget`, `ExtraHauler refused: Over budget`; the next ship, sol 40.55, carried them), and the old REPORT counted the asks (2 and 2) as orders. It now reports `asked of the ships` against `loaded on a manifest` (2 and 2 asked, 1 and 1 loaded; the pair that was needed), counted in `LoadCargo` when the manifest accepts the item, and the `INDUSTRY cargo` line says `asking the ship for` instead of `+N`. The roads, the life-support builds, the outpost's pylons, road stretches and drill, and the Forges of `IndustryStep` are all checked with `CanPlaceBuilding` / `CanPlaceRoad` (the sim's own tests) before they are ordered, and the Cargo Rover is held back unless it fits the ship, so those lines state an order the sim had just said it would take; none was ever refused in these runs (0 REJECTED lines in the full and lean industry runs). They are left as they were.
- Not changed (behaviour, not report): `IndSolarOwed` is still reduced when a Solar Array kit is put on the list, whether or not the ship takes it, and a refused Forge order is never retried (the order token is spent). Neither happened in these runs.

**Playtests** (`RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 ind_full guide full`, and `ind_lean ... lean`; 0 sim errors, 0 rejected orders). The HEAD industry=2 runs (`RH_PLAYTEST_INDUSTRY=2`, made with the unmodified binary) are the reference:

| Run | Pop at 110 | Recalled | Big Arrival | Q3 rounds met | Peak robot wear | Forges | Food made / drawn (kg a sol) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HEAD industry=2, full | 122 | 0 | sol 81.01 | 7 | 53 | 3 | 114.9 / 75.6 |
| **new default, full** | 122 | 0 | sol 81.01 | 7 | 53 | 3 (second standing sol 15.91, third sol 18.79) | 114.9 / 75.6 |
| HEAD industry=2, lean | 121 | 0 | sol 81.01 | 8 | 52 | 3 | 114.7 / 75.0 |
| **new default, lean** | 121 | 0 | sol 81.01 | 8 | 52 | 3 | 114.7 / 75.0 |

The bar passes on both: 0 recalled, the Big Arrival at sol 81 (by 110), 7 and 8 Q3 rounds (at least 3), peak wear 53 and 52 (below 100), and no robot halted or out of charge (stranded at once: 0). They reproduce the study. The report fix changes no sim behaviour: `diff` of each new run against its HEAD industry=2 run shows only wording (the start line; the `Forge 2/3 ordered` milestones replaced by `order accepted` and `running`; the `INDUSTRY cargo` verb; the `earth:` and `industry program:` REPORT lines), no number in any per-sol line.

**Opt-out and no-district identity.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 RH_PLAYTEST_INDUSTRY=0 ... 110 ind0_full guide full` against `Saved/baselines/e3b_full_playtest.txt`: the same 1,243 lines except the second-Forge report, which is exactly the false report (the new run has 1,244 lines):

A run without districts (50 sols, guide, full) is byte-identical to the same run on the unmodified HEAD binary (`cmp` clean, 301 lines; the second Forge never triggers there, Struct held the program back 2 sols).

### UI polish pass (P1-P8): what it settles, and where it deviated (director, 2026-10-24, not yet committed)

The presentation polish the design (`docs/qa/2026-10-24/ui-polish-design.md`, against `b5f5863`) set out, built on `bd0bc98` (`72e338a` above it adds a review document only), with the director's answer L7 (a): the accordion ships. Presentation only. `Source/RedHopeSim` is untouched, there is no save change (v53), no config row, no asset; the `RedHopeSim` dylib's sha256 is `394b17bc...3dd08010` before and after. The optional art job Q waits on L3 and L4 and is not started. Gate-D wording: rows S1-S4 in `docs/qa/2026-10-15/gate-d-wording.md`.

The design numbers the items P1-P8 in the order below; the brief that asked for the build listed them in another order (shaft buttons, section labels, turning labels, the first-shape hint, the accordion, the click-test waits, then the label background, the tram legend and the district label), so here is each by its design number.

**P1, the click-test flake (`RHClickTest.cpp`).** `FRunner::Tick`: a gap ends on the wall clock AND after 2 rendered frames (`GFrameCounter`), and a polling step's last try needs 30 frames as well as its timeout. `PushCheck`: a check that polls waits at least 3 s (it polls, so a passing run is no slower), and a failed check says `[waited N frames, longest frame M ms]`, so a flake (few frames, one long one) reads differently from a defect. Added beyond the design after one overloaded run (below): a press now follows its button (`ReAim`: the locator's widget is found again at the `down` and the `up`, and the pointer moves if the layout moved it), and the `down` line logs the leaf under the pointer.

**P2, the shaft strip's white buttons (`RHCommandDeck.cpp`).** `Next shaft` and `Fit a lift car` take `RHDeck::ButtonStyle()` and `RHDeck::Text`; the fit button shows only the verb, the reason (the sim's `CanFitLift` words) is a dim line under it (`GetFitLiftReason`, hidden while a car can be fitted) and its tooltip. The real text fields were looked at (`ui_after_E`): the roster search and the district name are already dark boxes with light text, so they are not restyled. The new `deck.contrast` step (end of the run, with a second shaft sunk so `Next shaft` shows) compares every visible deck button's fill (the brush it is drawn with now x its tint) with its text's colour in linear luminance and fails under 0.4.
- **Deviation, the before-run found three, not two.** On the HEAD binary plus only the new check: `Next shaft` (fill 1.00, text 0.62, diff 0.38), `Fit a lift car` (white; 0.34 as drawn hovered, 0.09 unhovered) and `-7` (the next floor to bore: fill 0.04, text 0.32, diff 0.29, unreached floors' text colour 0.28/0.33/0.37). The check was not loosened and nothing was exempted: the unreached floor text is lifted to 0.45/0.52/0.56 (diff 0.47; still dimmer than a reached floor's 0.55/0.66/0.70). After: 58-59 buttons compared, worst difference 0.47.

**P3, the accordion (`RHCommandDeck.h/.cpp`, `RHCommandDeckPanels.cpp`).** The five foldable panels (Politics, Crew, City, Fleet, Power) toggle through `FoldToggle` -> `SetFoldOpen`: opening one folds the others and, two frames later (the fold is laid out by then), `ScrollDescendantIntoView(TopOrLeft)` takes its header to the top of the column, asked every frame for a second while the panel's body fills. `Debug_SetCityExpanded` (`RH.District city 1`) is the same rule (it used to fold only Crew and Fleet). The roster's own toggle inside Crew is not one of them. The right column's scroll bar is 8 px (was 4). Nothing is saved; the defaults are as before. Tags for the test: `RH.CityToggle` (and one per panel), `RH.RightColumn`; `RH.Click <tag>` presses a tagged button. Click test: `city.open` (the harness opens Crew, takes the column to its end, a real click on City's toggle: City open, Crew folded, the header inside the column) and `city.scroll` (from the column's top, where City's header is below the fold, opening City brings the header on screen).

**P4, the TRAFFIC legend over the shaft strip (`RHCommandDeck.cpp`, `RHCommandDeckPanels.cpp`, `RHSectionVisualizerSubsystem.cpp`).** `GetStripLayout`: the strip stays centred on the left edge unless the top-left stack (vitals, uplink queue, legend) would reach it, then it stands 8 px under the stack, and its floor list drops from 11 rows to the rows that fit above the command bar. In the section the legend is one sentence plus only the tram lines the cut does not show; the long text (every line) is its tooltip (`GetLegendFullText`). Click test: `layout.legend` (the legend's and the strip's rectangles, both tagged, do not intersect), and `section.tram` now checks the short text does not name the drawn line and the full text does.

**P5, the first-shape hint (`RHPlayerController.cpp`).** `FloorHasDistrictShape` is a const read of `GetDistricts()` (cached by the districts' version and the floor). The tip adds `First shape on floor %d: its commutes will now walk through the digs and tunnels.` while the floor has none, every shape tip adds `A shape breathes its floor's air.`, and the confirm adds `(floor %d's commutes now walk its digs)` for the floor's first shape. Click test: `shape.hint` (pointer on the floor, brush armed: the hint holds "First shape on floor -1", the commute line and the air line) and `shape.again.hint` after `shape.land` brings the order home at 8x (it lags 45 sim-s): the second drag's tip, two cells off the first shape, is a shape tip with no "First shape". (The design named the existing `shape.again` step, which only cycles the Underground button before the brush is armed, so the hint checks are new steps beside it.)

**P6, labels that turn with the camera (`RHLabelTurn.h/.cpp`, the four layers).** One helper: `CameraQuarter` (the camera's yaw snapped to a quarter turn), `QuarterUp`, `FlatRotation`. Each layer's `Tick` re-aims its labels only when the quarter changes (cavern, tunnel, district, growth; no rebuild, no new component); the district kind word keeps below the name. The cavern label moves to the box edge nearest the camera for that quarter, 6.5 m out (the screen's bottom edge; it was 6.5 m past +Y, toward the right column at the opening yaw). Click test `labels.yaw`: the camera at yaw 90, every cavern and tunnel label's letters within 1 degree of the camera's up (0, 1) and the cavern label behind its box's middle; the harness puts the same cavern box and tunnel in at once (their orders lag), as `section.setup` already does for the cavern.

**P7, a shape's name on rock (`RHDistrictVisualizerSubsystem.cpp`).** The name goes to the nearest point that is dug (`FindFloorCell`), the nearest point as before when none is.

**P8, the section's labels (`RHSectionVisualizerSubsystem.cpp`, `RHPlayerController.cpp`, `RHCommandDeckPanels.cpp`, `RHStrategyPawn`).**
- (a) `GetFreeBand` on the deck reads the strip's right edge and the right column's left edge; `FrameSection` frames the slab and its labels into that band and centres them in it (the pawn gains the forward zoom curve, `WidthAtZoomCm`). It is fitted again when TRAFFIC turns on or off (the arrows' room and the lift labels change what must fit).
- (b) `ComputeLabelRoomCm` measures the real labels with the engine's font (an unplaced text component), floor labels to the left (the arrows' room under TRAFFIC), the lifts' and trams' labels either side; the room is what the widest label needs, not a fixed 6 cells.
- (c) Floor label size: 300 cm, within a fraction of the floor pitch. **Deviation:** the design said 55% (220 cm); a label's box measured 1.02 x its size on screen, so 300 cm in a 400 cm pitch leaves a quarter of the pitch between boxes and 55% only made 20 floors' labels a third smaller. It is 75% (300 cm at 400). The tram and robot labels go from 170 to 220 cm (the first click-test run drew the tram label 8.4 px tall).
- (d) The rock card reaches under the whole label column (worst-case figures, built once), and **deviation, found in the captures**: it also reaches 1000 cm lower, because the card stands rows behind the cut and the camera looks down 8 degrees, so the deepest floors' labels were drawn on the sand (light letters on tan). For the same reason SURF and the lifts' labels, which stand on the crust and sky, are drawn dark, and the floor labels pale (0.80/0.88/0.92).
- Click test `section.labels` (after `section.on`, and again after `section.traffic`): every drawn label's box (`GetLabelBoxes`, the box as drawn, vertical alignment included) is projected to the screen; inside the band between the shaft strip's right edge and the right column's left, none overlapping, none under 9 px tall (the em, the label's size projected). After: 7 labels, smallest 36.8 px; with TRAFFIC 9 labels, smallest 10.7 px.
- **What it cannot do:** at the 20-floor, three-shaft bench (`RH.DeepBench 3000`) at 1440x860 the slab and its labels fill the band at about 40 cm a pixel, so a floor label's letters are about 7.5 px (about 5 px of digit); D8's frame was about 36 cm a pixel (8.4 px). The labels are now where they can be read (not under the strip, on the card, apart) but they are small there, and 9 px would need the zoom to leave part of the slab out of the band, which the section cannot pan back. The 9 px bar is guarded in the click test's colony; the bench is for the director's eye.

### Economy fixes: the code review's findings 1-3 (2026-10-24)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-24/economy-code-review.md` (verdict PASS WITH FIXES), its should-fix findings 1-3. Finding 4 (the bot's false "second Forge ordered") was fixed in `bd0bc98`. Save **v54**. E4's design said "v54 if nothing lands first"; it now takes v55, and E5 and E6 move up one in turn. Each design already says the number is assigned at build time.

**What it builds.**
- **The frame (finding 2, the robust fix).** `RHSaveFrame` (`Public/RHSaveFrame.h`, `Private/RHSaveFrame.cpp`) is a shared helper. Each economy block at the end of the file is written in its own frame:
  - a header: `uint32 Tag, uint32 PayloadBytes, uint32 CRC32` (`FCrc::MemCrc32`);
  - the payload, written by its own `FMemoryWriter`;
  - a footer: `uint32 PayloadBytes, uint32 Tag`.

  The tags are `'RHE1'` (the ledger), `'RHE2'` (the credit) and `'RHE3'` (the export). Each reader finds its own frame in the file's bytes:
  1. where the outer reader stands, if the header and footer agree there;
  2. otherwise, by walking back from the end of the file (each footer names its header, and the 8 bytes before that header are the previous frame's footer).

  The CRC must then match. The payload is parsed by its own `FMemoryReader`, which can never run past it. So a block that is lost, cut short or misread can no longer move where the next block is read. The `bLedgerBlockLost` and `bCreditBlockLost` coupling is gone: a lost ledger no longer zeroes the credit. Names inside a payload go through `RHSaveFrame::ReadName`, which refuses a length outside 1-256 characters or longer than what is left, so a doctored length never allocates gigabytes or logs FString's own Error. E6's levers block can reuse the helper as it stands (`'RHL6'`; E6 7.3 asked for the CRC and the footer, and both are here).
- **The bounds (finding 2, the interim fixes, kept as defence in depth).** A loaded balance, awarded or spent total above `CreditMaxMcr` (10^12 cr) sets all three to 0 with a Warning; no real game comes near it. A loaded export price above the highest quote today's table can make is clamped to that quote, with a Warning. The highest quote is `GetEarthPriceCeilingMcrPerKg`: V x the multiplier at its clamp, 1 + `RequisitionEarthBonus`, less the launch cost. For Struct it is 4 cr/kg.
- **The ledger reader (finding 3).** These now reset the ledger with a Warning:
  - an open kg that is not finite or is below -0.001 kg (a real residual sits within half a gram of 0, so -0.0005 kg still loads);
  - a negative lifetime;
  - a checked sol other than C5d's `ProductionCheckedSol` (the two roll in lockstep from one `SolNow`);
  - a name whose `FName` repeats an earlier one (FName equality ignores case, so "glass" after "Glass" is a repeat);
  - bytes left over in the payload.

  A reset always rolls at `ProductionCheckedSol`, never at a value read from the bad block. `NoteFlow` ignores a non-finite amount.
- **The manifest (finding 1).**
  - **Locked prices.** `ManifestPricesMcr` holds one price per item aboard. `AddManifestItem` locks each item at today's table price. `GetManifestCostMcr` sums the locked prices, so a data change can no longer re-price a manifest that is already composed. `GetManifestTablePriceMcr` never returns below 0.
  - **Removal.** `RemoveManifestItem` takes the last such item off, with its locked price. Nothing is refunded, because nothing was charged: the launch is the only debit. It is open whenever the manifest is, and it is the way out of "Over budget at launch".
  - **The save.** The prices are saved at the end of the credit block (the count, then each price in manifest order). On load, any of these prices every item aboard from today's table, with a Warning: a lost credit block, a count other than the manifest's, a price outside [0, `CreditMaxMcr`], or bytes left over.
  - **The controls.** A REMOVE button on each picker row, enabled while that row is aboard. `RH.Manifest remove <Item>`. A click-test step, `manifest.REMOVE`.

**Behaviour that changes.** A data change between ADD and LAUNCH no longer refuses the launch. At v53 it did, and with no way to remove an item, the quota ladder stopped for good. A lost or malformed ledger block now leaves the credit as saved; at v53 the credit started at 0. In normal play nothing moves: the locked price is the table price, and the bot's log lines print the same numbers.

**Tests.**
- **`-economy`, 76 checks.**
  - EC1 compares the two saves up to the ledger's frame, and checks the credit and export frames are identical.
  - EC6 checks the frame layout, the byte-identical round trip and v53 refused. Its old doctored cases now reseal the CRC, so the block's own checks still judge them, and a count of -1 now leaves the credit as saved.
  - New in EC6: the review's count of 17, corrupted (the CRC catches it) and doctored (fill 6 and head 0x65746157, "Wate", read inside the frame); a count of 19; a name length one too long; the ledger's tag destroyed (the credit is found by the walk from the end); NaN and -5 kg open tallies; a -1 g lifetime; a checked sol of 1,000,000; "glass" after Glass; -0.0005 kg loads; `NoteFlow` ignores +Inf and NaN. Every case asserts the credit is exactly as saved.
- **`-credit`, 46 checks.**
  - CR7: the price lock (a re-price after ADD leaves 1,000 cr and the launch open), plus removal and its refusals.
  - CR9: the frame and the locked prices in the layout; v53 refused; 0x7530... mcr totals load as 0.
  - CR9, scenario A: a row re-priced to 50,000 cr after the save loads at its locked 1,000 cr.
  - CR9: one price for two items prices both from the table.
  - CR9, scenario B: a corrupted credit block loads at 0 and over budget at launch; removing both items lets the ship launch.
- **`-export`, 30 checks.** XE10 checks the frame, a 1,000,000 cr/kg lock clamped to 4 cr/kg, a count of 17 leaving bytes over, and a corrupted block lost with the credit kept.
- **Mutations**, each restored and checked by hash:
  - the CRC never failing fails 2 checks (CR9 scenario B, XE10);
  - no walk from the end fails 1 (EC6, the destroyed tag);
  - pricing from the live table fails 2 (CR7, CR9 scenario A).

  The first try at the CRC mutation (`false &&`) did not compile (`-Wunreachable-code` is an error), and the first try at the price mutation crashed the suite on a pointer bug in the mutation itself. Neither is a defect in the gate; both were redone.

**Battery** (`rh_battery.sh 10`, its logs newer than both dylibs): `BATTERY COMPLETE: 0 suite(s) not green`. Against `battery_post_ui`, `diff_battery.py` gives `TOTAL differing lines: 184`:
- 13 suites move only their version lines (53 to 54) or their file sizes; every save is 64 bytes longer (three 20-byte frames, plus the 4-byte price count);
- `-economy` 79 lines, `-credit` 41 and `-export` 22, the tests above;
- eraparity EP4 moves one wall-clock digit.

No sim line moved in any other suite: the `Manifest: +` lines in `-citygrowth`, `-arrivals` and `-resupply` print the same numbers, because the locked price is the table price. The pins hold. New baseline: `Saved/baselines/battery_post_ecofix`.

**Playtest.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 ecofix_full guide full` is byte-identical (`cmp`) to `Saved/baselines/ind_full_playtest.txt` (296,489 bytes, 1,732 lines, 0 sim errors).

**Click test** (1440x860). `manifest.ADD` and `manifest.REMOVE` passed in all nine runs. **On the final binary, runs 8 and 9 PASS 84/84** (83 plus `manifest.REMOVE`). Every failure fell on a step this gate does not touch, and only in runs whose log holds hundreds of `ZenLocal ... Insufficient Storage (507)` lines (200-1,010 a run; runs 1, 3 and 5 also logged them and still passed):
- Before the wrap fix below:
  - runs 1, 3 and 5 PASS 84/84;
  - run 2 failed `city.scroll` (997 ms frames);
  - run 4 hit the script's 240 s timeout.
- On the final binary:
  - run 6 failed `section.traffic.off` and `section.arm`;
  - run 7 failed a cascade of `shape.*` steps (310 ms frames).

The system volume had 2.0 GB free, so the shader cache could not write: each boot recompiled shaders, and the load average ran at 40-100. Runs 8 and 9 logged 0 such lines and passed. The UI polish runs earlier on 2026-10-24 logged none.

**Capture** (`Saved/RHCapture/ecofix`, 1440x860, looked at): every picker row has ADD, then REMOVE (greyed while that row is not aboard). The first capture showed the narrower name column clipping "Colonist Transport (20 colonists"; the name line now wraps (`ecofix_picker_wrap_00.png`), so long names take two lines and short ones stay on one.

**-scale.** 0 failures. Every digest equals `Saved/d6b_scale_1.log` (`5f9d7352`, `5c609384`, `1e609e47` x2, `a579cc22`, `aef9a16a`, `1c58c27d`, `c754e9f0`). Ledger: an agent step 0.2831 ms on against 0.2822 ms off; 1,000 rolls 0.045 ms. 1,000 `CanLaunchShip` calls 0.911 ms (budget 5).

**Checkpoints.** Re-made at v54: Midgame (sol 28.35) and Late (sol 62.35), 0 sim errors.
